| [commitlint.config.js](https://github.com/NVIDIA/NemoClaw/blob/main/commitlint.config.js "commitlint.config.js") | [commitlint.config.js](https://github.com/NVIDIA/NemoClaw/blob/main/commitlint.config.js "commitlint.config.js") | [fix(skills): delegate lifecycle to native agent state (](https://github.com/NVIDIA/NemoClaw/commit/133a1886c318a45adc0a979cc1e6ebfc407bf066 "fix(skills): delegate lifecycle to native agent state (#11093)  ## Outcome  Skill lifecycle state belongs exclusively to the selected agent. NemoClaw keeps no installed-skill inventory, receipt, provenance record, ownership ledger, mirror, or reconstructed list.  `nemoclaw <sandbox> skill list` always invokes the unmodified selected agent's native list command and streams its stdout, stderr, and exit status. Install and remove use an unmodified native command only when the pinned agent exposes an equivalent operation; otherwise NemoClaw performs one minimal stateless filesystem operation in the manifest-declared canonical writable user-skill root.  | Agent | Install | List | Remove | | --- | --- | --- | --- | | OpenClaw | Native `skills install` | Native `skills list` | Canonical-root removal only | | Hermes | Canonical-root placement only | Native `skills list` | Canonical-root removal only; native uninstall is hub-only and is not equivalent | | Deep Agents Code | Canonical-root placement only | Native `skills list` | Native `skills delete` |  NemoClaw never searches for or removes same-name bundled, project, plugin, global, or higher-precedence copies, and never claims that fallback placement is active or that fallback removal made a skill globally absent. The agent's native list and new sessions remain authoritative.  ## Reason  The previous implementation could mutate a NemoClaw-managed location while the selected agent listed or activated a different same-name skill. The attempted fix then introduced a second skill authority through receipts, provenance, compatibility patches, and reconstructed state. This change removes that split-brain boundary instead of maintaining it.  ### Related issues  Fixes #10210 Supersedes #10309  Maintainer scope decision: [NVIDIA MEMBER direction for OpenClaw, Hermes, and Deep Agents Code](https://github.com/NVIDIA/NemoClaw/pull/11093#issuecomment-5554314863).  ## Changes  - Deletes the OpenClaw, Hermes, and DCode skill compatibility patches and all managed-startup patch embedding/application checks. - Deletes the shadow skill registry/remote layer and its rebuild, destroy, snapshot, and cleanup wiring. - Removes Hermes' injected skill-reload tool and private cache mutation. - Stops creating or snapshotting DCode's obsolete `/sandbox/.deepagents/skills` shadow directory. - Adds static manifest metadata for each agent's native list command and canonical writable root, plus native add/remove argv only where equivalent operations exist. - Preserves the public `skill install` surface and adds the native `skill list` pass-through. - Uses the reviewed OpenShell TypeScript SDK for non-interactive native agent commands on NemoClaw-managed gateways. The pinned SDK has no upload/sync API, so the bounded local-tree transfer uses the existing provider-neutral OpenShell upload transport. - Uses OpenShell's reserved `host.openshell.internal` policy identity without Docker or Podman address lists or runtime branches. - Keeps DCode and the shared skill lifecycle targets on the canonical supported-runtime wildcard. - Removes 612 net lines (`2,589` additions, `3,201` deletions) against the current base.  ## Safety boundary  - Local input is limited to 1,024 files and 64 MiB. - Symlinks, special files, unsafe paths, and changed file identities are rejected; regular files are read without following symlinks. - Upload uses a private temporary snapshot and private sandbox staging directory. - Fallback placement/removal stays traversal-safe and relative to the validated canonical root. - Native stdout, stderr, and exit codes are forwarded without a NemoClaw post-publication state layer. - Staging exists only within the current invocation. NemoClaw does not record, retain, recover, reconcile, or otherwise treat it as durable state.  ## Runtime scope  - No Docker-versus-Podman branch exists in the skill implementation. - No Dockerfile build argument, image-context copy, or patch application was added. The only Dockerfile diff removes the obsolete DCode shadow-skill directory setup. - Portable has zero source diff and remains outside this change.  ## Verification  - Exact head: `b466297feca5f17307d3e5206a7e21fa05b6c2e4` - Exact base: `6d95c1ec0f5c872dc388891ab41b5337f4025ced` - `npm run validate:pr`, `npm run docs:strict`, focused unit/integration tests, and runtime-plan tests pass locally at the exact head. - Focused SDK, stateless action, manifest, policy, snapshot, lifecycle, mock-parity, and runtime-parity tests pass. - [Exact-head PR CI](https://github.com/NVIDIA/NemoClaw/actions/runs/34157827871) is green, including all 12 CLI shards, build/typecheck, static checks, package contracts, and installer integration. - [Exact-head managed-image validation](https://github.com/NVIDIA/NemoClaw/actions/runs/34157828019) is green, including OpenClaw, Hermes, and DCode direct startup, all-agent activation, DCode staging QA, and both OpenClaw MCP discovery passes. - [Exact-head advisors](https://github.com/NVIDIA/NemoClaw/actions/runs/34157826353) and CodeRabbit are green; there are no unresolved review threads. - [Exact-head Docker and Podman lifecycle E2E](https://github.com/NVIDIA/NemoClaw/actions/runs/34160676266/attempts/2) is green across all eight selected live jobs: - OpenClaw native lifecycle and fresh-session skill visibility pass on Docker and Podman.   - Hermes posture and skill lifecycle pass on Docker and Podman. - DCode passes on Docker and Podman through the same wildcard-selected implementation path. - DCode fresh re-onboard, Landlock, headless lifecycle, observability, public install, native list, fresh-session consumption, replacement refusal, native remove, post-remove non-consumption, and credential isolation pass on both runtimes. - Both DCode headless lifecycle artifacts report 22 passed and 0 failed. Attempt 1's Docker job had one exit-zero hosted-model response that missed the strict JSON/PONG envelope; its single failed-job rerun passed without code or assertion changes.  Original implementation and test commits remain attributed to @deepujain. Maintainer integration and follow-up commits remain attributed to @ericksoa.  --- Signed-off-by: Aaron Erickson <aerickson@nvidia.com>  ---------  Signed-off-by: Deepak Jain <deepujain@gmail.com> Signed-off-by: Aaron Erickson <aerickson@nvidia.com> Co-authored-by: Deepak Jain <deepujain@gmail.com> Co-authored-by: github-actions[bot] <41898282+github-actions[bot]@users.noreply.github.com>") [#11093](https://github.com/NVIDIA/NemoClaw/pull/11093) [)](https://github.com/NVIDIA/NemoClaw/commit/133a1886c318a45adc0a979cc1e6ebfc407bf066 "fix(skills): delegate lifecycle to native agent state (#11093)  ## Outcome  Skill lifecycle state belongs exclusively to the selected agent. NemoClaw keeps no installed-skill inventory, receipt, provenance record, ownership ledger, mirror, or reconstructed list.  `nemoclaw <sandbox> skill list` always invokes the unmodified selected agent's native list command and streams its stdout, stderr, and exit status. Install and remove use an unmodified native command only when the pinned agent exposes an equivalent operation; otherwise NemoClaw performs one minimal stateless filesystem operation in the manifest-declared canonical writable user-skill root.  | Agent | Install | List | Remove | | --- | --- | --- | --- | | OpenClaw | Native `skills install` | Native `skills list` | Canonical-root removal only | | Hermes | Canonical-root placement only | Native `skills list` | Canonical-root removal only; native uninstall is hub-only and is not equivalent | | Deep Agents Code | Canonical-root placement only | Native `skills list` | Native `skills delete` |  NemoClaw never searches for or removes same-name bundled, project, plugin, global, or higher-precedence copies, and never claims that fallback placement is active or that fallback removal made a skill globally absent. The agent's native list and new sessions remain authoritative.  ## Reason  The previous implementation could mutate a NemoClaw-managed location while the selected agent listed or activated a different same-name skill. The attempted fix then introduced a second skill authority through receipts, provenance, compatibility patches, and reconstructed state. This change removes that split-brain boundary instead of maintaining it.  ### Related issues  Fixes #10210 Supersedes #10309  Maintainer scope decision: [NVIDIA MEMBER direction for OpenClaw, Hermes, and Deep Agents Code](https://github.com/NVIDIA/NemoClaw/pull/11093#issuecomment-5554314863).  ## Changes  - Deletes the OpenClaw, Hermes, and DCode skill compatibility patches and all managed-startup patch embedding/application checks. - Deletes the shadow skill registry/remote layer and its rebuild, destroy, snapshot, and cleanup wiring. - Removes Hermes' injected skill-reload tool and private cache mutation. - Stops creating or snapshotting DCode's obsolete `/sandbox/.deepagents/skills` shadow directory. - Adds static manifest metadata for each agent's native list command and canonical writable root, plus native add/remove argv only where equivalent operations exist. - Preserves the public `skill install` surface and adds the native `skill list` pass-through. - Uses the reviewed OpenShell TypeScript SDK for non-interactive native agent commands on NemoClaw-managed gateways. The pinned SDK has no upload/sync API, so the bounded local-tree transfer uses the existing provider-neutral OpenShell upload transport. - Uses OpenShell's reserved `host.openshell.internal` policy identity without Docker or Podman address lists or runtime branches. - Keeps DCode and the shared skill lifecycle targets on the canonical supported-runtime wildcard. - Removes 612 net lines (`2,589` additions, `3,201` deletions) against the current base.  ## Safety boundary  - Local input is limited to 1,024 files and 64 MiB. - Symlinks, special files, unsafe paths, and changed file identities are rejected; regular files are read without following symlinks. - Upload uses a private temporary snapshot and private sandbox staging directory. - Fallback placement/removal stays traversal-safe and relative to the validated canonical root. - Native stdout, stderr, and exit codes are forwarded without a NemoClaw post-publication state layer. - Staging exists only within the current invocation. The only Dockerfile diff removes the obsolete DCode shadow-skill directory setup. - Portable has zero source diff and remains outside this change.  ## Verification  - Exact head: `b466297feca5f17307d3e5206a7e21fa05b6c2e4` - Exact base: `6d95c1ec0f5c872dc388891ab41b5337f4025ced` - `npm run validate:pr`, `npm run docs:strict`, focused unit/integration tests, and runtime-plan tests pass locally at the exact head. - Focused SDK, stateless action, manifest, policy, snapshot, lifecycle, mock-parity, and runtime-parity tests pass. - [Exact-head PR CI](https://github.com/NVIDIA/NemoClaw/actions/runs/34157827871) is green, including all 12 CLI shards, build/typecheck, static checks, package contracts, and installer integration. - [Exact-head managed-image validation](https://github.com/NVIDIA/NemoClaw/actions/runs/34157828019) is green, including OpenClaw, Hermes, and DCode direct startup, all-agent activation, DCode staging QA, and both OpenClaw MCP discovery passes. - [Exact-head advisors](https://github.com/NVIDIA/NemoClaw/actions/runs/34157826353) and CodeRabbit are green; there are no unresolved review threads. - [Exact-head Docker and Podman lifecycle E2E](https://github.com/NVIDIA/NemoClaw/actions/runs/34160676266/attempts/2) is green across all eight selected live jobs: - OpenClaw native lifecycle and fresh-session skill visibility pass on Docker and Podman.   - Hermes posture and skill lifecycle pass on Docker and Podman. - DCode passes on Docker and Podman through the same wildcard-selected implementation path. - DCode fresh re-onboard, Landlock, headless lifecycle, observability, public install, native list, fresh-session consumption, replacement refusal, native remove, post-remove non-consumption, and credential isolation pass on both runtimes. - Both DCode headless lifecycle artifacts report 22 passed and 0 failed. | [install.sh](https://github.com/NVIDIA/NemoClaw/blob/main/install.sh "install.sh") | [install.sh](https://github.com/NVIDIA/NemoClaw/blob/main/install.sh "install.sh") | [feat(installer): generalise deferred onboarding (](https://github.com/NVIDIA/NemoClaw/commit/11541cde94ceb5fb96670628ed6ae18e3be76257 "feat(installer): generalise deferred onboarding (#10341)  ## Outcome  Hermes and LangChain Deep Agents Code can install NemoClaw before NVIDIA inference credentials are available, then complete setup through their existing onboarding commands. Opted-in runtimes share one installer decision. Present credentials retain normal validation; absent credentials create no provider, sandbox, or completed onboarding state.  ## Reason  Deferred onboarding was implemented only for Hermes. Deep Agents Code needs the same separation between software installation and credential-dependent setup without another installer flow or onboarding state machine.  ### Related issues  Resolves #10297. The [maintainer decision accepts the scope, ownership, lifecycle, security, compatibility, and validation plan](https://github.com/NVIDIA/NemoClaw/issues/10297#issuecomment-5796678309).  ## Changes  - Add the manifest opt-in for Hermes and Deep Agents Code and use the existing installer plan with one provider-key classifier. Other runtimes retain their existing behavior. - Preserve current installer recovery order. Reject unsupported providers and local-model profiles before host preparation. Existing sandboxes use normal recovery; present or invalid credentials use normal onboarding validation. - Exercise the production resolver in integration tests and the public installer followed by the installed onboarding command in one shared live test, selected for both agents on Docker and Podman. - Preserve existing Portable Hermes startup receipts through the exact manifest transition for the added host-only flag. Retain startup arguments, source identity, state identity, descriptor checks, and historical receipt hashes. - Record the maintainer-approved, exact test-budget transition. Local and candidate CI recognize it; the independent GitHub check continues to consume only trusted-base policy. Existing live assertions and per-file budgets remain unchanged. - Update help and operator guidance, SDK installer fixtures, and the live-to-fast test mapping.  ## Verification  Commit: `a089420779264758d6cdc5e70c2ac4fef2f8211b`, source branch `NVIDIA/NemoClaw:feat/runtime-deferred-onboarding`.  - [Core PR CI](https://github.com/NVIDIA/NemoClaw/actions/runs/35900256877): passed, including all 12 CLI shards, installer integration, plugin tests, compilation, static checks, and coverage aggregation. - [Managed images](https://github.com/NVIDIA/NemoClaw/actions/runs/35900256837): passed, including OpenClaw, Hermes, and Deep Agents Code startup and all-agent activation on Docker and rootless Podman. - [Rootless Portable checks](https://github.com/NVIDIA/NemoClaw/actions/runs/35900257018): passed. - [Selected PR E2E](https://github.com/NVIDIA/NemoClaw/actions/runs/35900536475): all four deferred-install/onboarding cases passed. Every risk receipt names this exact commit, reports one executed passing test with zero skips, and has successful cleanup. The run reused the successful `ad3a461d` image contracts after verifying unchanged image inputs; its CLI and executed candidate are the current PR commit. [Detailed production-path evidence](https://github.com/NVIDIA/NemoClaw/pull/10341#issuecomment-5800447950). - Focused local checks passed: 141 CLI/agent/provider tests; 96 installer wiring tests; 93 preflight tests; 9 internal-CLI tests; 31 Portable contract tests; 9 OpenClaw integrity tests; 156 E2E support tests; 83 policy-boundary tests; the SDK handoff test; and 31 mapping-validator tests. - Documentation build: 0 errors, 2 warnings. Installer hash verification passed. All 11 PR commits are GitHub Verified; contributor history is preserved. - The inspected diff contains no secrets, API keys, or credentials.  ## Review notes  The independent `codebase-growth-guardrails` check reports the seven expected increases for the added live test. The [explicit maintainer exception](https://github.com/NVIDIA/NemoClaw/pull/10341#issuecomment-5799263232) covers only this exact budget transition. That failure is waived, not represented as passing. #12300 is closed; this remains one PR.  CodeRabbit's review of the current commit reports no actionable comments. All nine Advisor specialists completed. Five entries duplicate a product-authority finding based on a frozen `CONTRIBUTOR` association. Fresh GitHub reads identify the actual acceptance comment as `MEMBER` and independently confirm `ericksoa` has repository `admin` permission. The [maintainer disposition](https://github.com/NVIDIA/NemoClaw/pull/10341#issuecomment-5800651761) records that evidence and resolves the duplicate findings. The reported selector limitation is satisfied by the four passing executions from the PR workflow. No additional repository-change defect was identified.  The original maintainer review's acceptance and production-path evidence requests are addressed by the linked decision and live results. Independent documentation review also addressed the credential-presence wording. No retry, inference-validation bypass, or second onboarding state machine was introduced.  --- Signed-off-by: Tinson Lai <tinsonl@nvidia.com> Signed-off-by: Aaron Erickson <aerickson@nvidia.com>  ---------  Signed-off-by: Tinson Lai <tinsonl@nvidia.com> Signed-off-by: Aaron Erickson <aerickson@nvidia.com> Co-authored-by: github-actions[bot] <41898282+github-actions[bot]@users.noreply.github.com> Co-authored-by: Aaron Erickson <aerickson@nvidia.com>") [#10341](https://github.com/NVIDIA/NemoClaw/pull/10341) [)](https://github.com/NVIDIA/NemoClaw/commit/11541cde94ceb5fb96670628ed6ae18e3be76257 "feat(installer): generalise deferred onboarding (#10341)  ## Outcome  Hermes and LangChain Deep Agents Code can install NemoClaw before NVIDIA inference credentials are available, then complete setup through their existing onboarding commands. Opted-in runtimes share one installer decision. Present credentials retain normal validation; absent credentials create no provider, sandbox, or completed onboarding state.  ## Reason  Deferred onboarding was implemented only for Hermes. Deep Agents Code needs the same separation between software installation and credential-dependent setup without another installer flow or onboarding state machine.  ### Related issues  Resolves #10297. The [maintainer decision accepts the scope, ownership, lifecycle, security, compatibility, and validation plan](https://github.com/NVIDIA/NemoClaw/issues/10297#issuecomment-5796678309).  ## Changes  - Add the manifest opt-in for Hermes and Deep Agents Code and use the existing installer plan with one provider-key classifier. Other runtimes retain their existing behavior. - Preserve current installer recovery order. Reject unsupported providers and local-model profiles before host preparation. Existing sandboxes use normal recovery; present or invalid credentials use normal onboarding validation. - Exercise the production resolver in integration tests and the public installer followed by the installed onboarding command in one shared live test, selected for both agents on Docker and Podman. - Preserve existing Portable Hermes startup receipts through the exact manifest transition for the added host-only flag. Retain startup arguments, source identity, state identity, descriptor checks, and historical receipt hashes. - Record the maintainer-approved, exact test-budget transition. Local and candidate CI recognize it; the independent GitHub check continues to consume only trusted-base policy. Existing live assertions and per-file budgets remain unchanged. - Update help and operator guidance, SDK installer fixtures, and the live-to-fast test mapping.  ## Verification  Commit: `a089420779264758d6cdc5e70c2ac4fef2f8211b`, source branch `NVIDIA/NemoClaw:feat/runtime-deferred-onboarding`.  - [Core PR CI](https://github.com/NVIDIA/NemoClaw/actions/runs/35900256877): passed, including all 12 CLI shards, installer integration, plugin tests, compilation, static checks, and coverage aggregation. - [Managed images](https://github.com/NVIDIA/NemoClaw/actions/runs/35900256837): passed, including OpenClaw, Hermes, and Deep Agents Code startup and all-agent activation on Docker and rootless Podman. - [Rootless Portable checks](https://github.com/NVIDIA/NemoClaw/actions/runs/35900257018): passed. - [Selected PR E2E](https://github.com/NVIDIA/NemoClaw/actions/runs/35900536475): all four deferred-install/onboarding cases passed. Every risk receipt names this exact commit, reports one executed passing test with zero skips, and has successful cleanup. The run reused the successful `ad3a461d` image contracts after verifying unchanged image inputs; its CLI and executed candidate are the current PR commit. [Detailed production-path evidence](https://github.com/NVIDIA/NemoClaw/pull/10341#issuecomment-5800447950). - Focused local checks passed: 141 CLI/agent/provider tests; 96 installer wiring tests; 93 preflight tests; 9 internal-CLI tests; 31 Portable contract tests; 9 OpenClaw integrity tests; 156 E2E support tests; 83 policy-boundary tests; the SDK handoff test; and 31 mapping-validator tests. - Documentation build: 0 errors, 2 warnings. Installer hash verification passed. All 11 PR commits are GitHub Verified; contributor history is preserved. - The inspected diff contains no secrets, API keys, or credentials.  ## Review notes  The independent `codebase-growth-guardrails` check reports the seven expected increases for the added live test. The [explicit maintainer exception](https://github.com/NVIDIA/NemoClaw/pull/10341#issuecomment-5799263232) covers only this exact budget transition. That failure is waived, not represented as passing. #12300 is closed; this remains one PR.  CodeRabbit's review of the current commit reports no actionable comments. All nine Advisor specialists completed. Five entries duplicate a product-authority finding based on a frozen `CONTRIBUTOR` association. Fresh GitHub reads identify the actual acceptance comment as `MEMBER` and independently confirm `ericksoa` has repository `admin` permission. The [maintainer disposition](https://github.com/NVIDIA/NemoClaw/pull/10341#issuecomment-5800651761) records that evidence and resolves the duplicate findings. The reported selector limitation is satisfied by the four passing executions from the PR workflow. No additional repository-change defect was identified.  The original maintainer review's acceptance and production-path evidence requests are addressed by the linked decision and live results. Independent documentation review also addressed the credential-presence wording. No retry, inference-validation bypass, or second onboarding state machine was introduced.  --- Signed-off-by: Tinson Lai <tinsonl@nvidia.com> Signed-off-by: Aaron Erickson <aerickson@nvidia.com>  ---------  Signed-off-by: Tinson Lai <tinsonl@nvidia.com> Signed-off-by: Aaron Erickson <aerickson@nvidia.com> Co-authored-by: github-actions[bot] <41898282+github-actions[bot]@users.noreply.github.com> Co-authored-by: Aaron Erickson <aerickson@nvidia.com>") | 3 days agoSep 23, 2026 |

| [oxc.ignore-patterns.ts](https://github.com/NVIDIA/NemoClaw/blob/main/oxc.ignore-patterns.ts "oxc.ignore-patterns.ts") | [oxc.ignore-patterns.ts](https://github.com/NVIDIA/NemoClaw/blob/main/oxc.ignore-patterns.ts "oxc.ignore-patterns.ts") | [fix(lint): consolidate Oxc configuration and restore checks (](https://github.com/NVIDIA/NemoClaw/commit/2cb39b7f8100ac68aeafb4a911db8ec1b8a1814b "fix(lint): consolidate Oxc configuration and restore checks (#11402)  ## Outcome  Ordinary and type-aware lint now share one configuration. Plugin production and test files receive the intended typed checks, and maintained source under `build` directories is checked.  ## Reason  The separate typed configuration left several plugin rules inactive. Broad ignores and repeated formatter defaults obscured which files and rules were enforced.  ## Changes  - Remove the separate type-aware configuration and share common adapter/plugin rules. - Keep TypeScript discovery entries in the adapter and plugin source directories. Imported-source and Node-API fixtures prove single-file hooks resolve types; unrelated runtime bundle compilation keeps its existing behavior. - Remove `--tsconfig` arguments that did not select the type-aware program: [Oxlint uses separate discovery for type-aware programs](https://oxc.rs/docs/guide/usage/linter/cli.html). - Keep generated-output exclusions and remove unused formatter settings and repeated defaults. - Fix the newly exposed plugin findings, preserving empty and whitespace-only home-directory fallbacks. - Exercise the real lint hooks and ignored-path behavior with fixtures.  ## Verification  - `npx vitest run --project integration test/automation/lint test/cli/list-inference.test.ts` — 51 passed. The CLI listing suite also passed all 16 cases on exact canonical base `f6cfef3416a025141d75c1bf9ee1ac56061bc377`. - `npm run test:coverage:plugin` — passed within the broad gate; the revised `migration-state.test.ts` home-directory regression also passed all 62 focused cases. - Plugin production/test type-checks and all three lint passes — passed. - Reconciled commit: `npm run validate:pr`, `npm run lint`, CLI/plugin builds, and `npm run test:coverage:plugin` — passed. Plugin line coverage is 96.08%. - Managed startup bundle regeneration with the reviewed esbuild options — byte-identical to the committed artifact (`sha256:17ac7309b4f830947e0fcf88999c2e7b7e95cd67f880c3f6fccfac0aca2aeb6b`). - Initial `npm run check` at `322155d6e0efaf70bf5bbbe8af7b3dd392b571b8` — all-file hooks and plugin coverage passed; CLI coverage finished with 34,695 passed, 50 failed, and 83 skipped tests. - The diff contains no secrets, API keys, or credentials.  Across the stack, 36 failure names also reproduce on the recorded base `e94c06f130e1904229c9214f9d1f914b98f6b1ba`. All other full-run failure cases passed a focused coverage comparison of the initial stack head `180d5280719c96e3310b2de74983f86f56bfbba1` and that base: each had 247 passed, five identical installer failures, and one skipped case. The full runs used concurrent isolated containers; the local broad gate remains red.  Checks ran in disposable checkouts using Node 22.23.1, Oxlint 1.78.0, Oxfmt 0.63.0, and an 8 GiB Node heap. The container had no network or contributor credentials. Lint configuration and hook changes were tested as candidate code; validator byte identity with canonical main is not claimed.  ## Review notes  Stack 1/3; base: `main`. Self-review covered NVIDIA/NemoClaw commit `b88340f5e5ee5b8427482e09e410a929b57fc9f4`, including `.pre-commit-config.yaml`, `AGENTS.md`, and changed plugin blueprint/security files. Executable hook fixtures and the plugin suite support the changes. | [oxfmt.config.ts](https://github.com/NVIDIA/NemoClaw/blob/main/oxfmt.config.ts "oxfmt.config.ts") | [oxfmt.config.ts](https://github.com/NVIDIA/NemoClaw/blob/main/oxfmt.config.ts "oxfmt.config.ts") | [chore(lint): simplify Oxc config and remove useless escapes (](https://github.com/NVIDIA/NemoClaw/commit/209a02cab4f0223be867619f6fafa58adb722012 "chore(lint): simplify Oxc config and remove useless escapes (#11441)  ## Outcome  Oxfmt and Oxlint use fewer configuration entries and one combined type-aware pass. Lint now rejects unnecessary escapes after autofixing 256 findings across 54 files.  ## Reason  The merged Oxc migration left redundant defaults, duplicate invocations, and a repair-validation command that called a deleted formatter wrapper.  ## Changes  - Keep only shared exclusions in Oxfmt; remove redundant Oxlint plugin aliases, rule settings, and type-import options. - Combine adapter and plugin lint invocations and hooks. Keep typed autofixes before repository checks and preserve both TypeScript project boundaries. - Use native Oxfmt in focused repair validation, route typed files to type-aware lint, and reject invalid paths and symlinks before formatting. - Remove the `no-useless-escape` exemption after autofixing the findings. Add necessary/unnecessary escape cases to the existing lint fixtures. - Map the shared JSON parser to its live consumers and extend mapped fast tests for quoted Hermes requests, Windows process arguments, and nested JSON arrays.  ## Verification  - `npx vitest run --project integration test/automation/lint` — 66 tests passed, including typed hook ordering and escape enforcement. - Ordinary Oxlint and `oxlint --type-aware nemoclaw/src src/lib/adapters` — passed with no diagnostics. - `npm run format:check` — passed for 5,057 maintained source files. - `npm run build:cli` and `npm --prefix nemoclaw run build` — passed. - `npm run validate:pr` — passed; no tracked files changed. - `npm run check` on the initial commit — all repository-wide pre-commit hooks and plugin coverage passed. CLI coverage reported 34,707 passed and 192 failed tests; all 192 failures reproduced on base `915f34957c6b190603562541f8a6c33dc238837c` in the same isolated environment. This is not a full-suite pass. - Focused E2E-support tests (`launch-agent-turn` and platform parity) — 93 tests passed. - Fast tests for the repaired parity mappings — 94 tests passed. Growth guardrails — 45 tests passed. - `npx tsx scripts/checks/e2e-mock-parity.mts --base origin/main --head HEAD` — passed. - Compiler and regex AST comparison — all 54 autofixed files preserve parsed structure, decoded strings, and regex semantics. - Isolated repair-command exercises — passed mixed and single-scope routing, check/write modes, floating-promise rejection, deleted files, source extensions, invalid paths, and file/directory symlink rejection. - Reviewed the complete diff; no secrets, API keys, or credentials were added.  ## Review notes  Self-review covered NVIDIA/NemoClaw commit `053384c8bae5dce42174ebc42fef73b686ecabde`, including changed hooks, `.dsh` tooling, shell-generating runtime code, and security-related regexes. The AST comparison covers every autofixed file; source exercises cover the repair-validation change. The initial CI cycle found the missing fast-test parity; the mapped tests and committed parity check cover that repair. Independent review of sensitive paths is pending, so this PR is a draft.  DSH `tool_define` session validation was unavailable. Isolated Node exercises execute the changed source with the pinned Oxc binaries; the native DSH harness was not exercised.  Final PR validation used canonical base `ea68ea444a9f7e27455961ca0412258d47dca489`; the broad run used `915f34957c6b190603562541f8a6c33dc238837c` in the established isolated container with no contributor credentials. Dependency locks and validator executable hashes match the trusted validation environment. The candidate's lint configuration and hooks are the changes under test.  --- Signed-off-by: Carlos Villela <cvillela@nvidia.com>   <!-- This is an auto-generated comment: release notes by coderabbit.ai -->  ## Summary by CodeRabbit  - **Bug Fixes** - Corrected shell quoting and escaping in generated commands, improving handling of apostrophes, spaces, authentication, and environment values. - Strengthened validation for repository-relative paths and startup command tokens. - Improved generated zsh completion scripts when loading sandbox names.   - Expanded JSON detection to support arrays and nested values.  - **Refactor** - Streamlined formatting and linting checks across supported source areas. - Standardized regular-expression and command-string handling without changing existing validation behavior.  - **Tests** - Added coverage for command quoting, path safety, lint rules, JSON envelopes, and gateway argument handling.  <!-- end of auto-generated comment: release notes by coderabbit.ai -->  ---------  Signed-off-by: Carlos Villela <cvillela@nvidia.com>") [#11441](https://github.com/NVIDIA/NemoClaw/pull/11441) [)](https://github.com/NVIDIA/NemoClaw/commit/209a02cab4f0223be867619f6fafa58adb722012 "chore(lint): simplify Oxc config and remove useless escapes (#11441)  ## Outcome  Oxfmt and Oxlint use fewer configuration entries and one combined type-aware pass. | [package-lock.json](https://github.com/NVIDIA/NemoClaw/blob/main/package-lock.json "package-lock.json") | [package-lock.json](https://github.com/NVIDIA/NemoClaw/blob/main/package-lock.json "package-lock.json") | [fix(install): deliver the required OpenShell SDK without credentials (](https://github.com/NVIDIA/NemoClaw/commit/ef7cc9f876b9a9e94d80bdd69097dca59ddc2aba "fix(install): deliver the required OpenShell SDK without credentials (#11921)  ## Outcome  The installer and contributor setup install and verify the OpenShell SDK without GitHub credentials. Setup stops when the SDK cannot load, and the contributor doctor reports a missing or incompatible SDK.  ## Reason  A normal installation could silently omit the SDK from GitHub Packages and still finish, leaving commands such as `config export` unable to read sandbox inventory.  ### Related issues  Refs #11421. Refs #9818 for the broader SDK delivery work.  ## Changes  - Ship the reviewed SDK 0.0.116 archive and upstream license. Verify the existing lockfile SHA-512 before staging it in npm's cache. This gives the installer a credential-free source without requiring users to build the SDK. - Require SDK installation in the source installer, managed installer, and `dev:setup`; verify both SDK imports before setup completes or a managed installation is reused. `dev:doctor` checks the installed SDK without contacting a gateway. - Retain the existing optional npm metadata for isolated CI jobs, explicitly include optional packages in installer commands, and use a command-scoped SDK registry setting for npm 12. Include the SDK in packed CLI artifacts. - Share the existing CI cache-staging implementation and include the helper in its sparse checkouts. Package contracts cover offline installation, compiled SDK imports, archive corruption, and disabled lifecycle scripts; installer tests cover preparation/import failures and repair of an incomplete installation.  - Add sync/async MCP lock-storage tests for owner-preserving recovery, replacement races, symlinks, and directories. These cover the existing branch-coverage gate without changing lock behavior or lowering thresholds.  ## Verification  - Failing regression first: the contributor doctor returned success with the SDK missing; the assertion expected failure. It passes after the repair. - Contributor setup, reviewed archive, and CI cache preparation tests: 98 passed. The strengthened inherited npm-omit setting assertion also passed. - Existing compiled SDK-loading and packed CLI installation contracts plus the SDK installation contract: 5 passed. - Linux Node 22.23.2 with npm 10.9.8 and npm 12.0.2: SDK installation contract passed with network disabled, an empty npm cache, and no credentials. Both `npm ci` and `npm install` load the SDK despite inherited optional-package omission; lifecycle scripts do not run. Corrupted archives are rejected before cache creation. - Full installer integration lane: 710 passed, 2 skipped. After consolidating repeated fixture setup, the 8 affected installer tests passed again. - Isolated canonical-base publication validation: passed on Linux Node 22.23.2/npm 10.9.8. The validator and installed toolchain came from `fc8a1798d9b6d77c5a504e0d81cba9b1d29748ce`; it checked candidate `6a83162af02f76201c684a20e8ca52e37bb02208` without contributor credentials. CLI/plugin builds and CLI type checking passed. - Focused lock-storage coverage on macOS and Linux: 41 tests passed, 61.38% branches against the existing 60% minimum, and 100% functions. The selected suite failed the branch gate before the new recovery tests. Main integration checks passed (48 tests); test-project membership passed (2664 files across 7 projects). - Shell checks, formatting, and normal signed-commit hooks passed. The diff contains no secrets, API keys, or credentials. The archive checksum matches the existing reviewed lockfile value.  ## Review notes  Self-review covers NVIDIA/NemoClaw commit `6a83162af02f76201c684a20e8ca52e37bb02208`, including installer scripts, SDK cache/import helpers, the vendored archive, and CI sparse-checkout declarations. Archive verification precedes cache staging; SDK checks only load modules. The added state tests verify that failed recovery preserves the owner record and excludes unsafe filesystem objects. Independent review is pending.  --- Signed-off-by: San Dang <sdang@nvidia.com>   <!-- This is an auto-generated comment: release notes by coderabbit.ai --> ## Summary by CodeRabbit  * **New Features** * Added automatic preparation and verification of the bundled OpenShell SDK during setup and installation.   * Added offline SDK installation without requiring GitHub credentials.   * Added SDK availability and functionality checks in doctor mode. * Added support for securely staging and validating the bundled SDK package.  * **Bug Fixes** * Installations now stop with actionable errors when SDK preparation or verification fails.   * Reusable installations are repaired when the SDK is missing.  * **Documentation** * Added guidance for SDK archive installation, verification, troubleshooting, and updates. <!-- end of auto-generated comment: release notes by coderabbit.ai -->  ---------  Signed-off-by: San Dang <sdang@nvidia.com> Signed-off-by: Rebecca Sliter <571084+rsliter@users.noreply.github.com> Co-authored-by: Rebecca Sliter <571084+rsliter@users.noreply.github.com>") [#…](https://github.com/NVIDIA/NemoClaw/pull/11921) | last weekSep 16, 2026 |

| [tsconfig.runtime-preloads.json](https://github.com/NVIDIA/NemoClaw/blob/main/tsconfig.runtime-preloads.json "tsconfig.runtime-preloads.json") | [tsconfig.runtime-preloads.json](https://github.com/NVIDIA/NemoClaw/blob/main/tsconfig.runtime-preloads.json "tsconfig.runtime-preloads.json") | [test: backfill mockable coverage for live-only behavior + guard again…](https://github.com/NVIDIA/NemoClaw/commit/2d1eaf1f0db8f9b0d782e97476b5b98989d1252f "test: backfill mockable coverage for live-only behavior + guard against it (#6086)  ## Summary  Two regressions from #5874 (fixed in #6065) only surfaced in **live E2E targets that don't run on PR CI**. This PR closes that class of gap: it audits the live suite for behavior-critical assertions that are cheaply mockable, backfills them as fast units that run on **every** PR, and adds a guard so pure-unit blocks can't hide in live files again.  ## What's here  **1. The two direct #6065 regression fences** (mocked shell-units) - `reconcile`: an explicit `NEMOCLAW_MODEL_OVERRIDE` survives a divergent gateway model and the stale in-file fallback; normal drift-correction still runs when unset. - `guard recovery`: the restore warning is mirrored into `_NEMOCLAW_GATEWAY_LOG` (the marker the crash-loop E2E polls), and stays silent when the chain is healthy.  **2. High-priority mockable backfill** (security/recovery class) - ollama-auth-proxy: Bearer enforcement, no `/api/tags` bypass (#3338), header stripping, non-ASCII auth no-crash (#4820), backend 502. - `config get`: credential redaction + `gateway`-key omission (the `nvapi-` regression class). - device approval policy: scope-upgrade allowlist gate, gateway-env stripping, recover-failed rejection paths (#4462). - shields audit JSONL: credentials redacted before persistence. - hermes env secret boundary: value-shape (not key-name) discriminator; raw secrets rejected without echoing. - dashboard bind: `NEMOCLAW_DASHBOARD_BIND` opt-in incl. negative cases (#3259). - whatsapp compact QR: package shape-detection + terminal-only `small` (#4522).  **3. Medium/low backfill** - ollama token-file lifecycle (0600 / persisted / divergent-repair); extra-placeholder-keys canonical placeholder + accepted-keys breadcrumb; hermes `remove_stale_gateway_file` symlink-safety; token-rotation selective-rebuild naming; `_validate_port` fail-closed; snapshot `help` branch.  **4. Regression guard** - `scripts/checks/no-unit-blocks-in-live-e2e.ts` bans the vitest `it(...)` primitive inside `test/e2e/live/**` (that glob is uncollected on PR CI, so such blocks never run). Wired into the checks registry with its own unit test. - Relocated the existing offenders (skill-agent + messaging-compatible-endpoint classifier blocks, plus the bare-`test(` unit cases in common-egress + openclaw-inference-switch) into importable `test/e2e/support` modules with PR-collected tests; the live tests import them unchanged.  ## Notes  - Minimal behavior-preserving refactor to `whatsapp-qr-compact.ts` to export its pure helpers (the preload still auto-installs on require); `tsconfig.runtime-preloads.json` excludes the new co-located test from the shipped preload build. - `nemoclaw-start.sh`: made two possibly-empty-array iterations bash-3.2-safe via the existing `\"${arr[@]+...}\"` idiom so the shell-unit harnesses run on stock macOS bash. - **Deferred (2 low-value items):** the install.sh \"Resolved install ref:\" log assertion and the OpenClaw anthropic plain-baseUrl assertion both land in legacy-budget-capped test files where the growth guardrail forbids bumping the budget; the anthropic contract is already covered on the Hermes side and enforced live on the OpenClaw side.  ## Verification  - Every new/changed test verified green individually across the `cli`, `integration`, and `e2e-support` projects. - `npm run checks` (incl. the new live-unit-block guard), test-file-size budget, gitleaks, and CLI typecheck all pass. - The full `test-cli` pre-commit hook was skipped locally only because it trips on **pre-existing** macOS bash 3.2 failures in untouched shell-harness suites (`select`/`set -u`); CI runs bash 5.x, where they are green.  --- Signed-off-by: Prekshi Vyas <prekshiv@nvidia.com>   <!-- This is an auto-generated comment: release notes by coderabbit.ai --> ## Summary by CodeRabbit  * **Bug Fixes** * Improved startup robustness for environment parsing and background launch behavior. * WhatsApp compact-QR rendering more consistently uses the compact “terminal” style. * Dashboard remote bind activates only when explicitly opted in via `NEMOCLAW_DASHBOARD_BIND=0.0.0.0`. * Tightened audit/config redaction to prevent secret leakage and omit gateway details. * **Tests** * Expanded coverage for guard-chain recovery warnings, model override precedence, Hermes env-boundary hardening, and proxy/policy correctness. * **Chores** * Added a CI safeguard to prevent unit-test primitives from being included in live E2E tests. <!-- end of auto-generated comment: release notes by coderabbit.ai -->  ---------  Signed-off-by: Prekshi Vyas <prekshiv@nvidia.com>") | 2 months agoJul 6, 2026 |

Automatic selection requires one canonical package-service activation whose unit and environment prove that the service uses only port 8080.  ## Original Contribution  - Replays and supersedes contributor PR #10936 from @HOYALIM so trusted same-repository CI can run. - Preserves Ho Lim as the Git author of the original implementation commits. - Merges current `main` without rewriting contributor history.  ## Related Issue  Fixes #10824  Fixes #11231  ## Changes  - Reuse the installer's existing gateway-port validator for automatic candidates, including candidate-only exclusions for port 8080, the Hermes API range, and configured Hermes dashboard host ports. - Reject existing port-scoped state, matching OpenShell gateway registrations, active listeners, missing listener tools, and inconclusive probe results. - Canonicalize activation paths, resolve the effective `openshell-gateway.service` by systemd load precedence, reject applicable drop-ins and untrusted shadow units, and require exactly one `OPENSHELL_SERVER_PORT=8080` setting. - Preserve explicit port 8080 refusal and supported explicit non-default detached lifecycle behavior. - Persist a pending automatic identity before immediate or deferred onboarding, restore it for same-port recovery after interruption, promote it after success, keep no-name stop unscoped through automatic provenance, and preserve an explicit supported port as authoritative. The installer resolver is the single byte-level marker parser used by both installer and launcher boundaries. - Document automatic selection, failure conditions, and later-command port scoping.  ## Type of Change  - [ ] Code change (feature, bug fix, or refactor) - [x] Code change with doc updates - [ ] Doc only (prose changes, no code sample modifications) - [ ] Doc only (includes code sample changes)  ## Quality Gates  - [x] Tests added or updated for changed behavior - [ ] Existing tests cover changed behavior — justification: - [ ] Tests not applicable — justification: - [x] Docs updated for user-facing behavior changes - [ ] Docs not applicable — justification: - [x] Sensitive paths changed (security, policy, credentials, preflight, onboarding, inference, runner, sandbox, or messaging) - [x] Sensitive-path review completed or maintainer-approved waiver recorded — reviewer/approval link/justification: Accepted product authority and validation boundaries are recorded in #11231. the originally affected installer-integration files passed 99 tests with 1 expected skip, and the final status fix passed a focused preflight plus 124 additional installer-integration tests with 1 expected skip; 45 growth-guard tests passed. - [x] Applicable broad gate passed — `npm run checks:repository`, `npm run typecheck:cli`, and `npm run docs` passed. - [x] Quality Gates section completed with required justifications or waivers - [x] No secrets, API keys, or credentials committed - [x] `npm run docs` builds without errors - [x] Doc pages follow the style guide - [ ] New doc pages include SPDX header and frontmatter (new pages only)  ---  Signed-off-by: Aaron Erickson <aerickson@nvidia.com>   <!-- This is an auto-generated comment: release notes by coderabbit.ai --> ## Summary by CodeRabbit  - **New Features** - Automatically selected gateway ports are saved after successful onboarding and restored by later commands when no explicit port is provided. - Alternate-port selection works when the system service manager is unavailable.   - Explicitly configured ports remain authoritative.  - **Bug Fixes** - Improved validation for invalid, conflicting, ambiguous, or unverifiable gateway configurations.   - Failed or cancelled onboarding no longer saves a selected port. - Automatic port restoration no longer authorizes unnamed sandbox stops.  - **Documentation** - Expanded guidance on persistence, fallback behavior, upgrades, service validation, and troubleshooting. <!-- end of auto-generated comment: release notes by coderabbit.ai -->  ---------  Signed-off-by: Ho Lim <subhoya@gmail.com> Signed-off-by: Aaron Erickson <aerickson@nvidia.com> Co-authored-by: Ho Lim <subhoya@gmail.com>") | 2 weeks agoSep 9, 2026 |

# NVIDIA NemoClaw: Reference Stack for Sandboxed AI Agents in OpenShell

[Permalink: NVIDIA NemoClaw: Reference Stack for Sandboxed AI Agents in OpenShell](https://github.com/NVIDIA/NemoClaw#nvidia-nemoclaw-reference-stack-for-sandboxed-ai-agents-in-openshell)

[![License](https://camo.githubusercontent.com/d8e3b9d3b1213595b06824a404d41ad7743f1e880a3f9e47bae54533a9b60c99/68747470733a2f2f696d672e736869656c64732e696f2f62616467652f4c6963656e73652d4170616368655f322e302d626c7565)](https://github.com/NVIDIA/NemoClaw/blob/main/LICENSE)[![Security Policy](https://camo.githubusercontent.com/d7b3ea7a80c2502d6f91036a7b8d1b56bf3f0c7fda63638c5735c06d64af5644/68747470733a2f2f696d672e736869656c64732e696f2f62616467652f53656375726974792d5265706f72742532306125323056756c6e65726162696c6974792d726564)](https://github.com/NVIDIA/NemoClaw/blob/main/SECURITY.md)[![Discord](https://camo.githubusercontent.com/32172cbf5d9e1bad5a24c341e72d774dfdeb83e7099e33bfbb21756a3e669e42/68747470733a2f2f696d672e736869656c64732e696f2f62616467652f446973636f72642d4a6f696e2d373238396461)](https://discord.gg/XFpfPv9Uvx)

NVIDIA NemoClaw is an open source reference stack for running supported AI agents more safely inside [NVIDIA OpenShell](https://github.com/NVIDIA/OpenShell) sandboxes.
It provides guided onboarding, managed inference, network policy, managed integrations, snapshots, and lifecycle operations through the NemoClaw CLI and its agent-specific aliases.

**Supported agents:**

- [OpenClaw](https://openclaw.ai/) (default)
- [Hermes](https://get-hermes.ai/)
- [LangChain Deep Agents Code](https://docs.langchain.com/oss/python/deepagents/code/overview)

For capabilities, architecture, security controls, and the full feature list, see the [NemoClaw documentation](https://docs.nvidia.com/nemoclaw/latest/).

## Get Started

[Permalink: Get Started](https://github.com/NVIDIA/NemoClaw#get-started)

### Start with Your Coding Agent

[Permalink: Start with Your Coding Agent](https://github.com/NVIDIA/NemoClaw#start-with-your-coding-agent)

Use the starter prompt when you want Cursor, Claude Code, Codex, Copilot, or another local coding agent to install NemoClaw with you.

**[Copy the NemoClaw starter prompt](https://docs.nvidia.com/nemoclaw/latest/user-guide/openclaw/home.html#from-your-coding-agent)**.

The prompt tells your agent to use NemoClaw docs and skills, ask one question at a time, run commands only with your approval, and keep secrets out of chat.

### Install Using the Installer in Your Terminal

[Permalink: Install Using the Installer in Your Terminal](https://github.com/NVIDIA/NemoClaw#install-using-the-installer-in-your-terminal)

Review [Prerequisites](https://docs.nvidia.com/nemoclaw/latest/get-started/prerequisites.html) before installing.
On a supported DGX or Windows Subsystem for Linux (WSL) host, press Enter at the `Run express install with these settings? [Y/n]:` prompt to use the recommended preset settings for that platform.
Express install mode installs OpenClaw by default.
If you accept, refer to [NemoClaw Quickstart with OpenClaw](https://docs.nvidia.com/nemoclaw/latest/get-started/quickstart.html).
Enter `n` if you want to choose Hermes or LangChain Deep Agents Code, a sandbox name, an inference provider, and a model interactively.
When connecting to a Hermes sandbox from a light terminal, NemoClaw may install a managed `nemoclaw-light` Hermes skin for readable assistant text; it removes that managed skin state again when the terminal no longer needs it and preserves any user-selected Hermes skin.

| Agent | Guide |
| --- | --- |
| OpenClaw (default) | [Quickstart with OpenClaw](https://docs.nvidia.com/nemoclaw/latest/get-started/quickstart.html) |
| Hermes | [Quickstart with Hermes](https://docs.nvidia.com/nemoclaw/latest/get-started/quickstart-hermes.html) |
| LangChain Deep Agents Code | [Quickstart with LangChain Deep Agents Code](https://docs.nvidia.com/nemoclaw/latest/user-guide/deepagents/get-started/quickstart.html) |

## Documentation

[Permalink: Documentation](https://github.com/NVIDIA/NemoClaw#documentation)

Refer to the following pages on the official documentation website for more information on NemoClaw.

| Page | Description |
| --- | --- |
| [Overview](https://docs.nvidia.com/nemoclaw/latest/about/overview.html) | What NemoClaw does and how it fits together. |
| [Architecture Overview](https://docs.nvidia.com/nemoclaw/latest/about/how-it-works.html) | High-level overview of the host CLI, agent integration layer, blueprint, sandbox lifecycle, and protection layers. |
| [Ecosystem](https://docs.nvidia.com/nemoclaw/latest/about/ecosystem.html) | How OpenClaw, OpenShell, and NemoClaw form a stack and when to use NemoClaw versus OpenShell alone. |
| [Architecture Details](https://docs.nvidia.com/nemoclaw/latest/reference/architecture.html) | Detailed description of agent integration structure, blueprint lifecycle, sandbox environment, and host-side state. |
| [Prerequisites](https://docs.nvidia.com/nemoclaw/latest/get-started/prerequisites.html) | Hardware, software, and supported platforms, with any platform-specific pre-setup. |
| [Choose an Inference Provider](https://docs.nvidia.com/nemoclaw/latest/user-guide/openclaw/inference/learn-and-choose/choose-inference-provider) | Supported providers, validation, and routed inference configuration. |
| [Network Policies](https://docs.nvidia.com/nemoclaw/latest/reference/network-policies.html) | Baseline rules, operator approval flow, and egress control. |
| [Customize Network Policy](https://docs.nvidia.com/nemoclaw/latest/network-policy/customize-network-policy.html) | Static and dynamic policy changes, presets. |
| [Security Best Practices](https://docs.nvidia.com/nemoclaw/latest/security/best-practices.html) | Controls reference, risk framework, and posture profiles for sandbox security. |
| [Sandbox Hardening](https://docs.nvidia.com/nemoclaw/latest/user-guide/openclaw/manage-sandboxes/configure-sandboxes/review-sandbox-hardening) | Container security measures, capability drops, process limits. |
| [CLI Commands](https://docs.nvidia.com/nemoclaw/latest/reference/commands.html) | Full NemoClaw CLI command reference. |
| [Troubleshooting](https://docs.nvidia.com/nemoclaw/latest/reference/troubleshooting.html) | Common issues and resolution steps. |

## Community

[Permalink: Community](https://github.com/NVIDIA/NemoClaw#community)

Join the NemoClaw community to ask questions, share feedback, and report issues.
NemoClaw is an alpha project, so maintainers review issues, discussions, and pull requests on a best effort basis without guaranteed response timelines.

| Need | Channel |
| --- | --- |
| Setup or usage questions | [GitHub Discussions](https://github.com/NVIDIA/NemoClaw/discussions) or [Discord](https://discord.gg/XFpfPv9Uvx) |
| Reproducible bugs | [GitHub Issues](https://github.com/NVIDIA/NemoClaw/issues) |
| Feature proposals | Start with [GitHub Discussions](https://github.com/NVIDIA/NemoClaw/discussions), then open an issue when the scope is clear |
| Examples | [Community Examples](https://github.com/NVIDIA/nemoclaw-community) · [Contribute an example](https://github.com/NVIDIA/nemoclaw-community/blob/main/CONTRIBUTING.md#add-a-new-example) |
| Current priorities | [Current Priorities](https://github.com/NVIDIA/NemoClaw#current-priorities) |
| Contribution help | [CONTRIBUTING.md](https://github.com/NVIDIA/NemoClaw/blob/main/CONTRIBUTING.md) |
| Security vulnerabilities | Use the private channels in [SECURITY.md](https://github.com/NVIDIA/NemoClaw/blob/main/SECURITY.md); do not open public issues |

## Contributing

[Permalink: Contributing](https://github.com/NVIDIA/NemoClaw#contributing)

We welcome contributions. See [CONTRIBUTING.md](https://github.com/NVIDIA/NemoClaw/blob/main/CONTRIBUTING.md) for development setup, coding standards, and the PR process.

Prepare a source checkout without creating a runtime sandbox:

```
./scripts/dev-setup.sh
```

Or ask a compatible coding agent to use the repository's contributor-onboarding skill:

> Set up this machine as a NemoClaw contributor and prepare it for a first PR.

The contributor path is separate from the end-user installer above.
The default and `--repair` modes change only repository-local dependencies, builds, and hooks.
Use `./scripts/dev-setup.sh --expose-cli` only when you explicitly want a host-visible development CLI.
Use `./scripts/dev-setup.sh --with-runtime` only when your change needs sandbox validation; that approved flow also opts into CLI exposure.

## Security

[Permalink: Security](https://github.com/NVIDIA/NemoClaw#security)

NVIDIA takes security seriously.
If you discover a vulnerability in NemoClaw, **DO NOT open a public issue.**
Use one of the private reporting channels described in [SECURITY.md](https://github.com/NVIDIA/NemoClaw/blob/main/SECURITY.md):

- Submit a report through the [NVIDIA Vulnerability Disclosure Program](https://www.nvidia.com/en-us/security/report-vulnerability/).
- Send an email to [psirt@nvidia.com](mailto:psirt@nvidia.com) encrypted with the [NVIDIA PGP key](https://www.nvidia.com/en-us/security/pgp-key).
- Use [GitHub's private vulnerability reporting](https://docs.github.com/en/code-security/how-tos/report-and-fix-vulnerabilities/configure-vulnerability-reporting/configuring-private-vulnerability-reporting-for-a-repository) to submit a report directly on this repository.

For security bulletins and PSIRT policies, visit the [NVIDIA Product Security](https://www.nvidia.com/en-us/security/) portal.

## Current Priorities

[Permalink: Current Priorities](https://github.com/NVIDIA/NemoClaw#current-priorities)

NemoClaw's current priorities are maintained here as a public orientation point for contributors and community members.
This list is not a delivery commitment, support promise, or fixed roadmap; priorities can change as maintainers respond to security, quality, platform readiness, and community feedback.

- Improve install and onboarding reliability across tested platforms.
- Strengthen sandbox hardening, credential handling, and network-policy defaults.
- Validate local and routed inference behavior for supported provider paths.
- Keep documentation, troubleshooting guidance, and agent skills aligned with supported workflows.

For specific scoped work, use [GitHub Issues](https://github.com/NVIDIA/NemoClaw/issues) and start broader proposals in [GitHub Discussions](https://github.com/NVIDIA/NemoClaw/discussions).
Security vulnerabilities must use the private reporting channels in [SECURITY.md](https://github.com/NVIDIA/NemoClaw/blob/main/SECURITY.md), not public issues.
